8/22](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/09/12/harbor-house-restaurant-update-elk-ca-7-7-8-22/)\n\n![](https://i0.wp.com/diningwithfrankie.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/07/IMG_8680.jpeg?resize=656%2C300&ssl=1)\n\n~ [hsnixon](https://diningwithfrankie.com/author/hsnixon/)  ~ [2 Comments](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/09/12/harbor-house-restaurant-update-elk-ca-7-7-8-22/#comments)\n\n![](https://i0.wp.com/diningwithfrankie.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/07/IMG_8845.jpeg?resize=656%2C492&ssl=1)\n\nexterior –**5600 *CA*-1, *Elk*, 95432**\n\nFrankie and I have visited Harbor House Restaurant before and it is on this site numerous times.   Every time I go back I think I’ll just eat there and not do another write-up but the food is so good I really hate not to share the photos.  We were lucky enough to dine there 2 consecutive nights so Chef Matthew Kammerer was kind to vary the menu some but other courses just need to be repeated – and you don’t mind.  He continues to win awards for his culinary talents with the restaurant now having 2 Michelin stars.  Their ranch is also now producing some incredible produce which he includes in the dishes.  The wine program continues to evolve under the capable leadership of Wine and Beverage Director, John Miller.  Both nights he managed to delight us with interesting choices from the area.  The final component of the experience is the staff, which are all terrific.  Friendly and knowledgeable (many of our other dining choices came from their suggestions) they make a 2-star Michelin meal feel like you’re dining in a friend’s home.  The hotel it’s in, is a lovely place to stay, if you get the chance.  My highest recommendation here – I’ll go again and Frankie hopes to see you there too. [Continue reading →](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/09/12/harbor-house-restaurant-update-elk-ca-7-7-8-22/#more-83724)\n\n### Share this:\n\n* [Share on Facebook (Opens in new window) Facebook](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/09/12/harbor-house-restaurant-update-elk-ca-7-7-8-22/?share=facebook&nb=1)\n* [Share on LinkedIn (Opens in new window) LinkedIn](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/09/12/harbor-house-restaurant-update-elk-ca-7-7-8-22/?share=linkedin&nb=1)\n* [Share on Tumblr (Opens in new window) Tumblr](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/09/12/harbor-house-restaurant-update-elk-ca-7-7-8-22/?share=tumblr&nb=1)\n\n### Like this:\n\nLike Loading…\n\n# [Sushi By Scratch, Montecito, 6/27/22](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/09/03/sushi-bar-montecito-6-27-22/)\n\n![](https://i0.wp.com/diningwithfrankie.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/IMG_9512.jpeg?resize=656%2C300&ssl=1)\n\n~ [hsnixon](https://diningwithfrankie.com/author/hsnixon/)  ~ [Leave a comment](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/09/03/sushi-bar-montecito-6-27-22/#respond)\n\n![](https://i0.wp.com/diningwithfrankie.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/IMG_7635.jpeg?resize=656%2C492&ssl=1)\n\nexterior – **1295 Coast Village Rd., Santa Barbara, CA., 93108**\n\nSushi By Scratch is part of the historic Montecito Inn and the concept of Chef Phillip Frankland Lee.  It is the only Michelin-starred restaurant in the area and serves an Omakase 17 courses to 10 diners three times in the evening.  It is part of the Scratch Restaurant group with different concepts in cities like Los Angeles, Miami and Austin. This dining experience starts 15 minutes prior in the hotel’s lobby with a welcome cocktail. (Parking is via hotel valet or they will direct you to the self-park lot).  With the meal you can order your own beverage or buy one of their pairing packages – house, sake, or wine – all $110.  We took the House pairing which served a combination of sake, cocktails and beer.   In hindsight, even though the pairings were well matched,  I would recommend getting your own bottle of something as the pairings felt over-priced.  Seating was comfortable and the 3 chefs preparing your bites were conversational and friendly.  The 16 items featured are on the wall in front and #17 is dessert.  Numbers by each entry are the price of each if there is time and you want another piece at the end of the meal.  The sushi is all outstanding and it is a fun experience that I recommend. [Continue reading →](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/09/03/sushi-bar-montecito-6-27-22/#more-83391)\n\n### Share this:\n\n* [Share on Facebook (Opens in new window) Facebook](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/09/03/sushi-bar-montecito-6-27-22/?share=facebook&nb=1)\n* [Share on LinkedIn (Opens in new window) LinkedIn](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/09/03/sushi-bar-montecito-6-27-22/?share=linkedin&nb=1)\n* [Share on Tumblr (Opens in new window) Tumblr](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/09/03/sushi-bar-montecito-6-27-22/?share=tumblr&nb=1)\n\n### Like this:\n\nLike Loading…\n\n# [Caruso’s, Montecito, CA, 6/26/22](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/08/29/carusos-montecito-ca-6-26-22/)\n\n![](https://i0.wp.com/diningwithfrankie.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/07/IMG_7040.jpeg?resize=656%2C300&ssl=1)\n\n~ [hsnixon](https://diningwithfrankie.com/author/hsnixon/)  ~ [2 Comments](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/08/29/carusos-montecito-ca-6-26-22/#comments)\n\n![](https://i0.wp.com/diningwithfrankie.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/07/IMG_7005.jpeg?resize=656%2C492&ssl=1)\n\nhotel – **1759 S Jameson Ln, Montecito, CA 93108**\n\nCaruso’s is in the Rosewood Miramar Beach Hotel on the ocean front of Montecito serving Italian-inflected coastal cuisine.  It is the fine dining option at the hotel.  The facility was complete around 2019 and definitely delivers the pampered dining experience.  They offer a 4-course tasting menu with at least 4 choices in each category and also a 10-course Chef’s Tasting menu with optional wine pairings. Executive Chef Massimo Falsini was not there but the kitchen was in the most capable hands of Chef de Cuisine Shibani Mone.  We chose the 4-course menu and had our own wine after a well-made Negroni cocktail.  The large white cloth-covered tables are well spaced and are serenaded by the sound of the waves coming ashore.  There are lots of outdoor heaters for when the sun goes down.  When you pull up to the hotel the valet will park your car and then be prepared for a bit of walk down to the beach front.  You’ll have to cross an active railroad track and finally down a flight of stairs, but it’ll be worth the trek, I promise. [Continue reading →](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/08/29/carusos-montecito-ca-6-26-22/#more-83385)\n\n### Share this:\n\n* [Share on Facebook (Opens in new window) Facebook](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/08/29/carusos-montecito-ca-6-26-22/?share=facebook&nb=1)\n* [Share on LinkedIn (Opens in new window) LinkedIn](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/08/29/carusos-montecito-ca-6-26-22/?share=linkedin&nb=1)\n* [Share on Tumblr (Opens in new window) Tumblr](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/08/29/carusos-montecito-ca-6-26-22/?share=tumblr&nb=1)\n\n### Like this:\n\nLike Loading…\n\n# [Bouchard Restaurant & Inn, Newport, 5/27/22](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/08/19/bouchard-restaurant-inn-newport-5-27-22/)\n\n![](https://i0.wp.com/diningwithfrankie.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/05/IMG_6389.jpeg?resize=656%2C300&ssl=1)\n\n~ [hsnixon](https://diningwithfrankie.com/author/hsnixon/)  ~ [Leave a comment](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/08/19/bouchard-restaurant-inn-newport-5-27-22/#respond)\n\n![](https://i0.wp.com/diningwithfrankie.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/05/IMG_6318.jpeg?resize=656%2C492&ssl=1)\n\nexterior – **505 Thames Street, Newport, RI, 02840**\n\nBouchard Restaurant has been in business over 20 years and also has a few rooms/suites to stay in.  They have a parking area but you need to go into the restaurant and get a pass to place in your car before parking there.  They have a dress requirement that is on their website as well as reminded about when they call to confirm your reservation.  No jeans, t-shirts, flip-flops, etc but I saw no enforcement of that whatsoever.  There were many casually dressed people in the dining room that were obviously regulars due to their rapport with the staff.  There were also ‘dressed-up’ people there but this was only the first odd bit of this place.  They have a full bar which is located adjacent to the check in desk.  Further inside is a medium to small dining area with small white cloth covered tables with full drape underneath.  Lighting is lowered, there is music in the background, carpet is on the floor and the ceiling is low.   They offer a tasting  menu which is 5 courses at the second seating.  Most courses have choices, some with an upcharge.  The food was average at best but the service was awful.  Slow and inattentive, it aggravated me and probably affected the way I perceived the food.  The captain spent little time with many tables and concentrated on the ones he knew.  It’s a fine line to walk, but it bothers me to see tables sit for 20 minutes with their menus still in their hands and staff carrying on conversations neglecting their job. [Continue reading →](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/08/19/bouchard-restaurant-inn-newport-5-27-22/#more-83055)\n\n### Share this:\n\n* [Share on Facebook (Opens in new window) Facebook](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/08/19/bouchard-restaurant-inn-newport-5-27-22/?share=facebook&nb=1)\n* [Share on LinkedIn (Opens in new window) LinkedIn](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/08/19/bouchard-restaurant-inn-newport-5-27-22/?share=linkedin&nb=1)\n* [Share on Tumblr (Opens in new window) Tumblr](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/08/19/bouchard-restaurant-inn-newport-5-27-22/?share=tumblr&nb=1)\n\n### Like this:\n\nLike Loading…\n\n# [Gracie’s, Providence, 5/26/22](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/08/14/gracies-providence-5-26-22/)\n\n![](https://i0.wp.com/diningwithfrankie.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/05/IMG_5789.jpeg?resize=656%2C300&ssl=1)\n\n~ [hsnixon](https://diningwithfrankie.com/author/hsnixon/)  ~ [Leave a comment](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/08/14/gracies-providence-5-26-22/#respond)\n\n![](https://i0.wp.com/diningwithfrankie.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/05/IMG_5616.jpeg?resize=656%2C875&ssl=1)\n\nexterior – **194 Washington Street, Providence, RI., 02903**\n\nGracie’s is a medium sized place in downtown Providence with Matt Varga as chef.  There is no valet or assigned lot, so allow some extra time if you need to hunt for street parking.  In addition to the nice sized white cloth covered table they had a number of booth like seats around the perimeter.  Large windows to the street make up one wall of the dining room and they are heavily tinted which darkens the room quite a bit.  Patterned cut outs are on some of the windows and gauzy drapes hang between some of the perimeter tables.  There are a few tables outside.  The wood floor is opposite the high dark ceiling with hammered copper inserts in some of the wood columns and booth dividers.   The view out the window is of the street that is mostly filled with a bank building and its sign.  They offer an a la carte menu as well as 2 tasting menus of 5 or 7 courses.  Optional wine pairings are available and they have a full bar.  We had pre-ordered the 7 course tasting and ordered our own wine.  They promised to email and menu but never did.  It is a good place anyway. [Continue reading →](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/08/14/gracies-providence-5-26-22/#more-83049)\n\n### Share this:\n\n* [Share on Facebook (Opens in new window) Facebook](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/08/14/gracies-providence-5-26-22/?share=facebook&nb=1)\n* [Share on LinkedIn (Opens in new window) LinkedIn](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/08/14/gracies-providence-5-26-22/?share=linkedin&nb=1)\n* [Share on Tumblr (Opens in new window) Tumblr](https://diningwithfrankie.com/2022/08/14/gracies-providence-5-26-22/?share=tumblr&nb=1)\n\n### Like this:\n\nLike Loading…\n\n[Dining With Frankie](https://diningwithfrankie.com/)\n\n[IMG\\_1959](https://diningwithfrankie.com/about/img_1959/) / [Proudly powered by WordPress](https://wordpress.org/) Theme: Penscratch.\n\nLoading Comments...\n\n ",

      "url": "https://www.rimonthly.com/diners-update-7",
      "title": "Diner’s Update - Rhode Island Monthly",
      "content": "The former Quito’s Restaurant at 411 Thames St. in Bristol now has new owners and will be called Q’s Waterfront by O.M.G., a locally operated restaurant concept led by hospitality professionals Frank Martucci, Adam “Opie” Oppenheimer and Anthony Guaetta. Together, the partners plan to create a modern New England waterfront dining experience featuring a seafood-forward menu, quality ingredients and a broad selection of non-seafood offerings, complemented by a creative beverage program. [...] Becky’s BBQ in Middletown is closing at the end of the month but will continue catering, it announced on social media posts. “We will still be offering catering for your parties, events, gatherings, and special occasions. We’re excited to continue serving you in a new way! For now, you can reach out to us through Big Mike’s Smokehouse, by phone at ☎️ 401-841-9909.”\n\nLe Central bistro in Bristol closed on Sept. 3., according to the Providence Journal. Chef Jesse James owned the restaurant for more than twenty years.\n\nThe Grind, the coffee shop at Crook Point Brewing in Providence, closed on Sept. 26. [...] The Mount Hope Farm farmers market continues from 9 a.m. to 12:30 p.m. at Mount Hope Farm in Bristol each Saturday. Head over for your weekly groceries, courtesy of local farmers and artisans like Barden Family Orchard, Roots Farm, Brightside Seafood, Navad Bakers and Two Gals Cocktails. Need a dose of cuteness? Be sure to visit the farm animals during your visit. There are pre-ordering options as well as the ability to buy on site.",
      "score": 0.07092566,
      "raw_content": "Social Media\n\n# Diner’s Update\n\n##### The latest Rhode Island food, drink, restaurant and dining news and events.\n\n[Jamie Coelho](https://www.rimonthly.com/bios/jamie-coelho/),\n\n|  |\n| --- |\n|  |\n\nThe official ribbon cutting at the West End Kitchens in Providence.\n\n## OPENINGS AND UPDATES\n\n[**Hope & Main**](https://www.makefoodyourbusiness.org/west-end-kitchens/) has officially launched the West End Kitchens in Providence. The expansion allows food entrepreneurs and small food and drink businesses better access to commercial kitchens to launch, scale and compete in the marketplace. The new kitchens at 945 Westminster St. have already kicked off thirty-five food and beverage businesses since opening its doors on June 26. Stop by the West End Makers Markets, held every Friday throughout October from 4–7 p.m. at 945 Westminster St., where you can learn about the new businesses and sample their goods for sale.\n\n[**FeedRI**](https://feedri.org/) and Ocean State Food Pantry, a ministry of Legacy Church, have partnered to open a new affordable community market at 211 Buttonwoods Ave. in Warwick. The market offers groceries and household essentials at significantly reduced or no cost for individuals and families facing food insecurity, with a shopping environment designed to preserve dignity and choice. The Warwick market is modeled in part after FeedRI’s Hope Market in Rumford and builds on the organization’s broader work across Rhode Island, including food rescue, school-based pantries and other efforts to make nutritious food more accessible.\n\n[**Ocean House**](https://www.oceanhouseri.com/) in Watch Hill recently renovated its restaurant, COAST, and it’s now offering more flexible menu options at the Forbes Five-Star restaurant. Guests can choose from three-course, four-course and Chef’s Tasting menus, with savory and vegetarian options showcasing seasonal ingredients from local farms and select purveyors.\n\nThe latest [**Nitro Bar**](https://www.instagram.com/p/Dd61iDkkbzf/?img_index=1) is now open at 134 Ives St. in Providence, where Glou used to be.\n\n[**Dave’s Hot Chicken**](https://daveshotchicken.com/) opened its newest location at 1200 Fall River Ave. in Seekonk, Massachusetts.\n\n[**La Fe Taqueria**](https://www.instagram.com/lafetaqueria/) opened in the former Blue Cottage space on Hope Street in Providence. The business is partially owned by the the owner of Lotus Pepper.\n\n[**The Peach Cobbler Factory**](https://www.instagram.com/p/Dc_XPuLgWzQ/) has also opened at 102 Dean St. on Providence’s Federal Hill, bringing the viral desserts to Providence from the Southern-inspired chain. “Think warm fruit cobblers served with ice cream, creamy banana pudding, loaded cinnamon rolls, giant cookies, brownies, churro stix, thick milkshakes and their signature Pudd-N-Shakes.”\n\nA Portuguese-inspired coffeehouse has opened in Barrington. Maggie Mulvena, founder of Feast & Fettle meal delivery service and author of *The Feast & Fettle Cookbook*, has created [**Aldeia Coffeehouse**](https://www.aldeiacoffeehouse.com/) as a space to celebrate Portuguese heritage through coffee drinks, pastries, sandwiches, European pantry items and ceramics. It’s open Wed.-Mon. from 7:30 a.m. to 4 p.m. (closed Tuesdays).\n\n[**Kingfish**](https://www.instagram.com/kingfishwarren/) in Warren is open. Walk-ins only. They are offering $2 shucks on Mondays. The seafood-focused restaurant is open Thursday through Monday, starting at 4 p.m.\n\nNewport Harbor Island Resort on Goat Island has revamped its [**1639**](https://www.newportharborisland.com/dine/1639/) dining concept, and hired a new director of food and beverage, John Barth. Barth is now handling 1639, room service, banquets and catering, and the resort’s four other dining concepts: Torpedo Bar & Lounge, The Firepits, The Bakery and The Pineapple Club. 1639 offers a refined coastal dining experience that celebrates the flavors of New England with an innovative twist, including house-made pastas and locally sourced seafood and meats.\n\n## TRANSITIONS\n\n[**Ten Prime Seafood**](https://www.tenprimeseafood.com/) restaurant is closing later this fall in the former Bravo and Res space in Providence. But that’s not the end of [**Chow Fun Food Group**](https://www.chowfunfoodgroup.com/) in the building. The restaurant will become a new concept later this fall, transforming into CUT 123. [**Read more**](https://www.rimonthly.com/cut-123/).\n\n[**Not Just Snacks**](https://www.instagram.com/p/DeeqLJqx8rJ/) on Hope Street in Providence experienced a fire yesterday. Fortunately, the fire was contained to the roof and no one was injured. The business is open as usual.\n\nDebbie’s in downtown Providence has closed, but the space will become [**Ello’s Cafe**](https://www.instagram.com/elloscafe/), a new business operated by Cindy Lopez and the team at Viva Mexico.\n\n[**Newport Restaurant Group**](https://www.newportrestaurantgroup.com/) is developing a new restaurant concept for the former Applebee’s space at Garden City Center in Cranston. The restaurant at 24 Midway Rd. will be the group’s nineteenth location and its second at Garden City, where Avvio has operated since 2016. The concept is still in development, and NRG says the restaurant will create dozens of jobs within its 100 percent employee-owned company.\n\nMaven’s Delicatessen will launch wholesale operations in late October after expanding into the space next door to its Pawtucket location. A new baking facility will produce its breads, bagels and cookies, allowing the business to grow. [**Read more**](https://www.rimonthly.com/mavens-to-expand-operations/).\n\nThe former Quito’s Restaurant at 411 Thames St. in Bristol now has new owners and will be called [**Q’s Waterfront by O.M.G.**](https://www.facebook.com/qswaterfront/), a locally operated restaurant concept led by hospitality professionals Frank Martucci, Adam “Opie” Oppenheimer and Anthony Guaetta. Together, the partners plan to create a modern New England waterfront dining experience featuring a seafood-forward menu, quality ingredients and a broad selection of non-seafood offerings, complemented by a creative beverage program.\n\nThe [**Boat House**](https://www.boathousetiverton.com/) in Tiverton is closed for renovations, and will reopen in the spring of 2027. When it reopens, guests can look forward to a refreshed dining room and bar and outdoor expansion. The revitalized space will feature a brand-new outdoor bar on the North Patio with panoramic views of Mount Hope Bay, as well as additional seating and a new awning on the South Patio.\n\nThe former Newport Creamery in Barrington has been purchased by the Blount family and will reopen as Barrington Creamery. According to an [**Instagram post**](https://www.instagram.com/p/DcKLQEOHdfE/) from the town of Barrington, the restaurant will reopen in 2027. “Barrington Creamery is a community restaurant opening in 2027 at 296 County Road — built for, and with, the people of Barrington and the East Bay. On the menu: the classic creamery favorites you grew up with, a few newer and lighter options for the way people eat today, and select highlights pulled from the Blount menu. It’s a mix that’s meant to feel familiar the first time you walk in. This is a neighborhood place first — for families, regulars, kids after school, and everyone in between. The Blount family will be managing the establishment, but it is dedicated to local families; and Creamery diehards!”\n\n[**DeQuattro Restaurant Group**](https://dequattrogroup.com/) is adding another restaurant to its roster, this time, down in South Kingstown. They already own Pane e Vino on Federal Hill in Providence, two Massimo locations, one on Federal Hill and another at Legacy Place in Dedham, Mass., the Crow’s Nest in Warwick and Bar ‘Lino in Johnston. They recently purchased the Chophouse Grille location at 191 Old Tower Hill Rd., and will call this new restaurant Acqua Pazza, according to Gail Ciampa at the [***Providence Journal***](https://www.providencejournal.com/story/entertainment/dining/2026/08/24/dequattro-group-to-open-acqua-pazza-in-wakefield-as-6th-restaurant/91345740007/?gnt-cfr=1&gca-cat=p&gca-uir=true&gca-epti=z116408p001250n00----l001250c00----e006900v116408d--77--b--77--&gca-ft=219&gca-ds=sophi&utm_campaign=entertainmentgreeting&utm_content=nprj-providence-nletter04&utm_medium=email&utm_source=providencejournal-gails-food-feed-strada&utm_term=newsletter-greeting).\n\nSpeaking of Italian, Pat’s Italian in [**Cumberland**](https://www.instagram.com/p/DcwdbHNyw64/) may have closed, but Pat’s Italian is coming to Providence’s Federal Hill in the space that was formerly occupied by Blackstone’s on the Hill. In the meantime, you can still visit [**Pat’s locations**](https://www.rimonthly.com/pats-italian/) in Coventry, Warwick and Johnston.\n\n## CLOSINGS\n\nClementine, the cocktail bar owned by David Cicilline, has closed. A message posted on [**Instagram**](https://www.instagram.com/p/DeABRn5pxD7/) from the Clementine team stated: “After four great years, Clementine has closed its doors. We would like to take this time to thank every one of you who walked through our doors, made our home your home, your stop before dinner at one of our neighbors, or your nightcap after a show at Trinity Rep. We couldn’t have asked for better company these 4 years. Keep your eyes on 200 Washington Street over the following weeks to see what amazing things will happen next at this truly special little spot, and thank you again.”\n\n[**Sin Bakery**](https://www.eatwicked.com/) will be closing permanently on October 31, which they call “the perfect day for a bakery called Sin!” “Thank you for everything you’ve given us. It’s meant so much to be a part of your lives and celebrations for the last twenty years,” wrote owner Jennifer Luxmoore.\n\n[**Debbie’s**](https://www.instagram.com/debbies_ri/) sandwich shop in downtown Providence has closed. Owner Ed Davis wrote on Instagram: “Why are we closing? Well that’s about a million different reasons but the big one is it just wasn’t busy enough.”\n\nBecky’s BBQ in Middletown is closing at the end of the month but will continue catering, it announced on [**social media posts**](https://www.instagram.com/p/DdMemMJCJR9/). “We will still be offering catering for your parties, events, gatherings, and special occasions. We’re excited to continue serving you in a new way! For now, you can reach out to us through Big Mike’s Smokehouse, by phone at ☎️ 401-841-9909.”\n\nLe Central bistro in Bristol closed on Sept. 3., according to the ***[Providence Journal](https://www.providencejournal.com/story/entertainment/dining/2026/09/09/le-central-in-bristol-closed-by-retiring-owner-jesse-james/91597713007/?gnt-cfr=1&gca-cat=p&gca-uir=true&gca-epti=z114708p115450n00----l115950c00----e004500v114708d--57--b--57--&gca-ft=255&gca-ds=sophi&utm_source=providencejournal-gails-food-feed-strada&utm_medium=email&utm_campaign=entertainmentgreeting&utm_term=newsletter-greeting&utm_content=nprj-providence-nletter04).*** Chef Jesse James owned the restaurant for more than twenty years.\n\n[**The Grind**](https://www.instagram.com/p/DdFb7H6kd-n/?hl=en&img_index=1), the coffee shop at Crook Point Brewing in Providence, closed on Sept. 26.\n\nThe Puerto Rican cafe [**Little Sister**](https://www.littlesisterpvd.com/) announced it is closing at the end of the year after eight years in business on Hope Street in Providence. They will continue brunch, but service is now first-come, first-served. Owner Milena Pagan posted on the the Little Sister Instagram account, announcing the decision: “I want to set the narrative straight from the jump: This decision is purely lifestyle driven,” Pagán wrote. “My Papi is getting very old, he’s 78. I haven’t spent significant time with him in the 20 years I’ve been studying and working in America. That’s a long time to be away from your family. I need more of my time back so I can visit Puerto Rico more often, build our family farm … and pursue other opportunities that are coming up in our lives. We are so grateful for the experience — the beautiful memories we’ll treasure, and the unsavory moments that ultimately made us stronger.”\n\n## **EVENTS, SPECIALS AND MENU UPDATES**\n\n**10/16, 10/23, 10/30**\n\nAttend the Hope & Main West End Makers Markets on Fridays from 4 to 7 p.m. through October 30 at the Providence location on 945 Westminster Street.\n\n**10/16-10/17**\n\nCastle Hill Inn in Newport is welcoming back Michelin-starred chef Nick Curtin of Copenhagen’s acclaimed [**Alouette**](https://www.restaurantalouette.dk/), to once again join forces with [**Aurelia at Castle Hill**](https://aurelianewport.com/) and chef Quentin Diez for a special multi-course tasting experience on Oct 16 and 17. Earlier this year, Aurelia at Castle Hill also received the coveted 2026 Forbes Travel Guide Five-Star Award. Originally from Providence, chef Nick Curtin is returning to his home state for this special two-night celebration. Copenhagen’s Alouette highlights artisans and local growers and he’s bringing this same thoughtful approach to Aurelia at Castle Hill. Guests will enjoy a curated prix-fixe menu that celebrates Rhode Island’s local bounty – from peak-season produce to the region’s renowned seafood and artisanal purveyors. Reservations will be available via [Open Table](https://shared.outlook.inky.com/link?domain=ldpr-dot-yamm-track.appspot.com&t=h.eJxNkE1vgjAAQP_KwnkVWghUT4KAis6Z4cfgYioFoVLB8hVY9t8Xlh12fYeXvPclNSKXZi9SWtdlNZPlnJYC0KIGPeEc1IJE9wkpy6os6klUcBkpHPqd8dGq7apRr0egLJXuxFbWORBwj6LeprpyCO2AOdV733isMJfOylxYru8GuHp-xo9bKc7rhXG8WlU6MGvP2DZ0QAtjslOX68bm_Y0kKLT53nQRE2YeZ4fL1tQHDyxEl2_PbTZtHfDEyXMfV1f43OXYP9c0HDptunkL7Muh1emmMtwmKk7JCYopdy6h16LBt7IH6Dxv6zPp9UW6j-UiA7x41Gneyywq4jwt5iL7I7_JCdRoAqNIg4RECEJsGLpKoI5pDFGMFRkaGKsYIYgmGtIUfVTHo5pw8qBkPj4dTSOnI_8P6nE__P4BFyp-KA.MEUCIQDHUhNV5Jo2aKQvFBtHr4yYGDQrM-4u_ValkmWiPyNW5AIgG35OiPw75mTFTKCmUX_oismSxEzoN-1_5xqBEkDPMP4 \"https://shared.outlook.inky.com/link?domain=ldpr-dot-yamm-track.appspot.com&t=h.eJxNkE1vgjAAQP_KwnkVWghUT4KAis6Z4cfgYioFoVLB8hVY9t8Xlh12fYeXvPclNSKXZi9SWtdlNZPlnJYC0KIGPeEc1IJE9wkpy6os6klUcBkpHPqd8dGq7apRr0egLJXuxFbWORBwj6LeprpyCO2AOdV733isMJfOylxYru8GuHp-xo9bKc7rhXG8WlU6MGvP2DZ0QAtjslOX68bm_Y0kKLT53nQRE2YeZ4fL1tQHDyxEl2_PbTZtHfDEyXMfV1f43OXYP9c0HDptunkL7Muh1emmMtwmKk7JCYopdy6h16LBt7IH6Dxv6zPp9UW6j-UiA7x41Gneyywq4jwt5iL7I7_JCdRoAqNIg4RECEJsGLpKoI5pDFGMFRkaGKsYIYgmGtIUfVTHo5pw8qBkPj4dTSOnI_8P6nE__P4BFyp-KA.MEUCIQDHUhNV5Jo2aKQvFBtHr4yYGDQrM-4u_ValkmWiPyNW5AIgG35OiPw75mTFTKCmUX_oismSxEzoN-1_5xqBEkDPMP4\") beginning September 16. The prix fixe menu will be available for $185 per person, with beverage pairings and wines available at an additional cost.\n\n**10/16-10/18**\n\nTrack 15 will host a three-day [**Harvest Fest**](https://track15ri.com/events/harvest-fest-friday/) Fri., Oct. 16 through Sun., Oct. 18, with food, drinks, live music, family activities and seasonal events throughout the weekend. Highlights include live music [**Friday night**](https://track15ri.com/events/harvest-fest-friday/), pumpkin painting, kids’ crafts, an artisan market, tarot readings and a Moniker Brewery tap takeover featuring a traditional Bierstacheln or “hot pokies” session. A family-friendly Halloween movie will also be shown indoors. [**Saturday**](https://track15ri.com/events/harvest-fest-saturday/) will feature dozens of artisan vendors, pumpkin painting, Roger Williams Park Zoo’s Creepy Crawlies from 1-3 p.m., a kids and family costume contest at 4 p.m. and a full WaterFire lighting beginning at 6 p.m. The festivities continue [**Sunday**](https://track15ri.com/events/harvest-fest-sunday/) with the artisan market, pumpkin painting contest voting and giveaways, plus the Patriots game on TVs throughout the food hall and plaza followed by Halloween movies.\n\n**10/17**\n\n[**Fearless Fish Market**](https://www.fearless-fish.com/)‘s Oystermania is taking place Oct. 17 from noon to 5 p.m. at 721 Hope St. in Providence. Fearless Fish will have oysters from three growers: East Beach Blondes, Fox Island, North Star. Food will be provided by Little Friend, ice cream by Big Feeling, music by Playing Dead, beer with Moniker Brewery, a kid zone and more!\n\n**10/17**\n\nNarragansett Brewery in Providence is hosting [**Neighbor Day**](https://www.facebook.com/events/s/narragansett-neighbor-day/1583385443278621/) on Sat., Oct. 17 from 1–6 p.m. Co-hosted with Providence Flea, Neighbor Day is a celebration of the community and will feature local vendors, live music, a beer garden, games, taproom food specials and local organizations. They will also have free pet portraits from 2–4:30 p.m., with suggested donations benefiting the Providence Animal Rescue League.\n\n**10/17**\n\nRun through the Vines at Sakonnet Vineyard in Little Compton. Local runners will gather at Sakonnet Vineyard for the annual [**Rhode Races Wine Run**](http://rhoderaces.com/events/wine-run). The scenic three-mile trail run takes participants through vineyard paths and rolling terrain, finishing with a celebration of wine, food and live music. Runners will receive a souvenir wine glass and enjoy post-race festivities including a barbecue and drinks among the vines. Bib pick-up begins at 11 a.m., with the race starting promptly at 1 p.m., followed by a post-race BBQ at 3:30 p.m. Tickets for the BBQ and wine only are also available. For more information and to register, visit **[rhoderaces.com/events/wine-run](https://rhoderaces.com/events/wine-run/)**.\n\n**10/18**\n\nGet ready to embrace your love for garlic and apples at the [**Tiverton Farmers Market’s**](https://www.tivertonfarmersmarket.com/) annual Garlic & Apple Roast on Sun., Oct.18, 2026, from 10 a.m. to 2 p.m. at Tiverton Middle School at 10 Quintal Dr. in Tiverton. Stop by the market for a day filled with flavor, fun and community spirit while browsing fifty fantastic local and regional vendors. **La Gatta Salata Food Truck** will be serving up delicious Italian sandwiches, sides, focaccia and sauces. Explore fresh, locally sourced offerings from **Little Rhody Pasta**, featuring pasta & ravioli infused with vibrant garlic flavors. Enjoy Mediterranean delights from **Salty Wine Bar** and mouthwatering dishes from **Tolia Anatolian** **Eats** including hummus, Muhammara, olives, whipped feta and more.\n\n**10/21**\n\n[**Mother Pizzeria**](https://www.motherpizzeria.com/) is bringing back its Pizza Oven Guest Chef Takeover Series this fall, with the first one kicking off on Wed., Oct. 21 from 5-9 p.m. The first one in the series will be with the restaurant, [**Dolores**](https://dolorespvd.com/), from Providence. The Dolores team will feature one snack and one pizza, alongside Mother’s regular menu. [**Reservations**](https://www.motherpizzeria.com/reservations/) are now open.\n\n**10/24**\n\nRoger Williams University teamed up with [**Tragmar Ale Works brewery**](https://www.tragmar.com/) in Bristol to turn oysters from its [**Ferrycliffe Shellfish Farm**](https://www.rwu.edu/academics/ceed/shellfish-program) into a limited-edition Oyster Stout. Ferrycliffe is part of RWU’s Center for Economic and Environmental Development (CEED), which supports sustainable aquaculture through research and education. The on-campus research farm works with local commercial shellfish farmers and gives students real experience across the industry, from the hatchery to maintaining shellfish on the dock. The Ferrycliffe Oyster Stout release party is Saturday, Oct. 24, from 1–4 p.m. at Tragmar in Bristol.\n\n**10/24**\n\n[**Munroe Dairy**](https://www.cowtruck.com/) will host its first Trunk-or-Treat on Sat., Oct. 24 from 3 to 6 p.m. at Sacred Cow Market and Cafe at 187 N Brow St. in East Providence. Families and local businesses are invited to decorate their vehicles and distribute candy to trick-or-treaters, play carnival games, take pictures with The Count from Sesame Street and more. Guests are welcome to visit the Sacred Cow to shop for their favorite Munroe Dairy items, including apple cider, coffee milk, iced coffees, cakes, cookies and seasonal treats.\n\n**10/24**\n\n[**The Summer’s End PVD Block Party**](https://track15ri.com/events/summers-end-block-party-waterfire/) at Track 15 was postponed due to bad weather at the end of September, but the good news is it’s now set for Sat., Oct. 24 from 5–11 p.m. Track 15 and RI Night Market will transform the property into a large outdoor market and entertainment space featuring fifty-plus Rhode Island and New England artisans, makers and creative businesses, along with food and drinks from Track 15 and live music and entertainment throughout the evening. The event will also coincide with WaterFire, making it a particularly busy night in downtown Providence.\n\n**10/30**\n\n[**The Vanderbilt, Auberge Collection**](https://auberge.com/vanderbilt/) in Newport is hosting its annual Halloween event, the [**Ghosts of the Atlantic: Drowned Pirates Ball**](https://auberge.com/vanderbilt/experiences/#!/event/019fa50e-aa83-763b-a481-515baabafd37), on Fri., Oct. 30, 2026 from 7-11 p.m. The event tells the tale of the seafaring legend of mansioneer Alfred Gwynne Vanderbilt and Newport’s storied harbor. Inside the historic mansion, candlelit halls will come alive with ghostly sailors, restless captains and haunted Gilded Age socialites, blending Newport’s rich history with a playful nautical twist. Guests can expect nautical cocktails, decadent passed hors d’oeuvres, stationed food and live entertainment throughout the evening. Costumes are encouraged, whether guests arrive as a bewitched deckhand, glamorous Gilded Age ghost or phantom captain. Tickets include an open bar, food and access to all entertainment. Open to hotel guests and local visitors for $275 a ticket, this twenty-one-plus event offers a unique way to celebrate Halloween inside one of Newport’s most iconic historic mansions.\n\n**11/13-11/14**\n\nThis fall, [**Ocean House**](https://www.oceanhouseri.com/) will welcome chefs from Twin Farms and Manoir Hovey for an exclusive two-night Relais & Chteaux culinary collaboration at COAST. Taking place November 13 and 14, the dinners will bring together the culinary identities of coastal Rhode Island, rural Vermont and Québec’s Eastern Townships through a collaborative six-course tasting menu. Highlights will include bluefin tuna tartlet, fried chicken with caviar, foie gras torchon, chitarra with local shellfish, Ocean House tautog, and Vermont Wagyu striploin. The dinner is priced at $245 per person, with an optional $175 wine pairing. Reservations can be made via [**OpenTable**](https://www.opentable.com/r/coast-at-ocean-house-westerly-2).\n\n**11/26**\n\nThis Thanksgiving, FeedRI will host its third annual Friendsgiving celebration on Thurs., Nov. 26 in partnership with Providence-based catering company [**Easy Entertaining.**](https://www.easyentertainingri.com/) FeedRI will transform Farm Fresh RI into a festive gathering space, offering thousands of Rhode Islanders who experience food insecurity a traditional Thanksgiving meal and the chance to celebrate the holiday together. Friendsgiving has become a meaningful annual tradition that ensures individuals and families experiencing food insecurity or hardship can enjoy Thanksgiving. Chefs Ashley Vansasse and Andre Lopez of Easy Entertaining will lead a team of volunteers to prepare the feast. Local businesses and community partners make the celebration possible, including Quality Food Company of West Warwick, [**Easy Entertaining**](https://www.easyentertainingri.com/) of Providence, [**Wright’s Creamery**](https://www.wrightsri.com/), Johnson & Wales University, Resmini Law, and numerous local restaurants, retailers and corporate supporters. [**Wright’s Dairy Farm & Bakery**](https://www.wrightsri.com/) will also donate freshly baked pies for guests to enjoy during the Thanksgiving feast.\n\n## **ONGOING EVENTS**\n\n[**India Restaurant**](https://indiarestaurant.com/?gad_source=1&gad_campaignid=16202085986&gclid=CjwKCAjw25fWBhAVEiwAMopNjqczETTvHEvtkcgccTInNJzIAjxSpX2TlB7RZl4_tS7FU6iI3DLCchoC6xcQAvD_BwE) is leading a fundraising initiative in partnership with HAPSA to support those affected by the devastating flash floods in northern Nepal following a catastrophic glacial collapse on August 26. Now through Oct. 15, customers can purchase $25 gift certificates from India Restaurant, with 100 percent of the proceeds donated to HAPSA, a 501(c)(3) nonprofit organization dedicated to strengthening emergency care and supporting communities affected by the disaster.\n\nOct. 1-31, [**Track 15**](https://track15ri.com/) is running **Lights On Plates Full**, a monthlong dine-for-a-cause program featuring special dishes from all seven of its vendors. For every participating plate purchased through Oct. 31, $1 will be donated to one of seven Rhode Island nonprofits supporting local youth. The participating dishes range from Little Chaska’s Pumpkin Curry and Dune Brothers’ Crispy Spicy Hot Fish Sandwich to There, There’s Rodeo Burger, with each vendor supporting a different local organization.\n\n[**Weekapaug \"https://shared.outlook.inky.com/link?domain=weekapauginn.com&t=h.eJxdjE0OgyAYBa9iWFeRqvy48iogn4WKYBDSmKZ3b2m66u5l8maeKEeHxgqZlPZjxPgBsMpd5pv1vpnDhpOBOsKRZI7Sp_qK0aVCa1Girbfgk3Envs8BnAlTtD_yVeUykLkDphkDznnXc9VrBrJVZFCECkwYF5S2VPQNY58lShtKW23ZHcZ6mDZ5Lhac3mNploMuhz_8egMBfEBJ.MEUCIFL3B5D8ASt-vevRsExqFw4n9vXGkh03J5dK1g6pbEyJAiEAmF4rkJUnW_7_H961QPaXPuJLsbEOjqvYBL9JgAw8klg\")**, and its research efforts. Created by Executive Chef Gerardo Viejo and his culinary team, the three-course prix fixe menu features seasonal dishes including pan-seared scallops with beet purée, crispy-skin salmon with cranberry and beurre rouge, and macaron framboise with fresh raspberries. The Pink Menu will be available Sunday through Thursday throughout October for $95 per person. Reservations are recommended and may be made by visiting [**weekapauginn.com**](https://shared.outlook.inky.com/link?domain=weekapauginn.com&t=h.eJxdzEsOgyAUheGtGMatSFQuOHIrINdC5WFQ05ime29pOurs5OTL_yRH9mSoiN33dRsofSAualXHzcVYTylQcqnIUkR215Dibv1J71NCb9OY3e_5SjX3bGoRDAAKIdpO6M4AqkazXjMuKQMhOW-47GqAz5KljaWtw-E36yKOQZ2zQ2_WXJoFmAL-7tcbTWU6BA.MEUCICwPbkcTtZK9N0lPWW9rgkxqMbDFDgEz-J4qdqfHsAucAiEAjcjSe46sX1xPUlrkHAGNiCYnEMZlXPFZvVDlaMP3FmQ \"https://shared.outlook.inky.com/link?domain=weekapauginn.com&t=h.eJxdzEsOgyAUheGtGMatSFQuOHIrINdC5WFQ05ime29pOurs5OTL_yRH9mSoiN33dRsofSAualXHzcVYTylQcqnIUkR215Dibv1J71NCb9OY3e_5SjX3bGoRDAAKIdpO6M4AqkazXjMuKQMhOW-47GqAz5KljaWtw-E36yKOQZ2zQ2_WXJoFmAL-7tcbTWU6BA.MEUCICwPbkcTtZK9N0lPWW9rgkxqMbDFDgEz-J4qdqfHsAucAiEAjcjSe46sX1xPUlrkHAGNiCYnEMZlXPFZvVDlaMP3FmQ\").\n\nGame day package at J.T. Commons.\n\n[**J.T. Commons**](https://www.jtcommonsrestaurant.com/) in Middletown is offering a Game Day Package for your next watch party. The package serves six to eight and includes a choice of two of J.T.’s signature focaccia pizzas, including the Just Cheese, Pepperoni, Sausage, Peppers & Onions or the RI Red with signature red sauce and grated parmesan; as well as two pounds of buffalo, barbecue or plain wings; and choice of spinach and artichoke dip, served hot with house-made corn tortilla chips, or French onion dip, served cold with house-made potato chips. Orders must be placed thirty-six hours in advance. Any order cancellations less than twenty-four hours before pickup time will be subject to a 25 percent charge. The cost is $95; tax and gratuity are additional.\n\nAt [**Beech**](https://www.beechjt.com/) in Jamestown, every Sunday is “Bolognese Sunday” where they bring back this beloved guest favorite dish exclusively on Sundays for $36. The dish is a traditional recipe from Bologna, Italy, made with a five-hour simmered sauce and housemade tagliatelle pasta.\n\n[**Ten Steak & Sushi**](https://www.tenprimesteakandsushi.com/) located at 55 Pine St. in Providence is offering a new Social Hour Monday through Friday from 4:30 to 6 p.m., which includes a full menu of elevated bar bites, $10 cocktails and half-priced wine. Small bites include Chips & Dip ($9) with the option to add caviar ($14); tenderloin yakitori with garlic mango sauce ($12); deluxe truffle fries ($10); jumbo shrimp cocktail with Old Bay and bloody mary cocktail sauce ($12 for four shrimp); tuna crispy rice: tuna, avocado, crispy rice, spicy mayo and sriracha ($10); California roll: lump crab, cucumber and tobiko ($10); spicy ahi tuna maki with cucumber, spicy mayo and togarashi ($10); and Rhode Island calamari with banana peppers and jalapeño tartar sauce ($14). $10cocktails include the Ten Prime Martini with 10 Prime Organic Vodka and blue cheese stuffed olives; Ten’s signature mojito with Bacardi Superior, muddled lime and mint; red or white sangria; and the Moscow mule with 10 Prime Organic Vodka, ginger beer and lime. A selection of wines are available for half-price during Social Hour, including Torresella Rosé Prosecco ($6), Kim Crawford Sauvignon Blanc ($6), Santa Margherita Pinot Grigio ($7), Carpineto Dogojolo Sangiovese ($6), the 2021 Cambria Santa Maria Valley Pinot Noir ($8), and the 2021 Ely Callaway Paso Robles Cabernet Sauvignon ($8).\n\n## FOOD TOURS\n\nGet a feel for the Providence and Newport dining scenes with Rhode Island Red Food Tours. For *Rhode Island Monthly* magazine’s Best of Rhode Island Award-winning [**Downcity Providence Food Tour**](https://www.rhodeislandredfoodtours.com/our-tours/downcity-providence-food-tour/), delicious adventure awaits where food, art, and architecture collide in Providence’s vibrant Downcity Arts District. Sample from diverse Rhode Island favorites and award-winning culinary restaurants, and between bites get an insider’s view of this lively and creative small city. Expert guides will help you discover the treasure of epicurean favorites, hidden art, historic architectural gems, sharing local stories and the many happenings to be found in Rhode Island’s Creative Capital. Tours take place on Thursdays, Fridays, Saturdays and Sundays, mid-April through November. [**Tickets**](https://www.rhodeislandredfoodtours.com/our-tours/downcity-providence-food-tour/) are $79 Per Person (no alcohol) or $99 Per Person (with alcohol), or $105 (no alcohol with *Rhode Island Food Crawls* book) or $125 (with alcohol and *Rhode Island Food Crawls* Book).\n\nDuring the Rhode Island Red Food Tours’ [**Newport Neighborhood Tour**](https://www.rhodeislandredfoodtours.com/our-tours/newport-neighborhood-food-tour/), guests will discover diverse, eclectic out-of-the-way neighborhood eateries – with a homespun vibe, where the locals know to go as well as hotbeds of food and fun in the more traveled parts of the city. Step inside some of what may appear as a hole-in-the-wall café or an authentic, cozy 18th century tavern and you will be surprised to find a mouthwatering food scene, using both cultural & regional influences with a strong commitment to sustainable ingredients. Tours take place on Thursdays, Fridays, Saturdays and Sundays, mid-April through November. [**Tickets**](https://www.rhodeislandredfoodtours.com/our-tours/newport-neighborhood-food-tour/) are $79 Per Person (no alcohol) or $99 Per Person (with alcohol), or $105 (no alcohol with *Rhode Island Food Crawls* book) or $125 (with alcohol and *Rhode Island Food Crawls* Book).\n\nProvidence Tour Company has launched a new culinary experience, [**The Federal Hill Dinner Stroll.**](https://www.pvdtourco.com/federal-hill-dinner-stroll/) This guided, multi-course dining adventure takes guests through the heart of Federal Hill, highlighting its Italian cuisine with a guide every step of the way. Guests will enjoy a lavish four-course Italian dinner, starting with antipasto, followed by housemade pasta, a savory entrée, and finishing with cannoli. This culinary journey takes place while strolling between four of Federal Hill’s most iconic restaurants: Cassarino’s, Trattoria Appia, Pane e Vino, and Anthony’s Authentic Italian Cuisine. Priced at $100 per person and available on Thursdays through the end of the year, this intimate experience spans approximately 2.5 to 3 hours and includes food, taxes and gratuities; all you’ll need to add is your choice of drink along the way. Complimentary valet parking is available at the starting point, Cassarino’s Restaurant (177 Atwells Ave.), and the walk covers roughly one mile.\n\n## NOTABLES\n\n*Condé Nast Traveler* announced the results of its prestigious 2026 Readers’ Choice Awards. This year’s **[regional list](https://www.cntraveler.com/gallery/new-england-top-hotels)** included [**Castle Hill Inn**](https://www.castlehillinn.com/) in Newport at number one, the [**Watch Hill Inn**](https://www.watchhillinn.com/) in Westerly at number four, [**The Vanderbilt, Auberge Collection**](https://auberge.com/vanderbilt/) in Newport earned the tenth spot, downtown Providence’s luxury boutique property, [**The Beatrice**](https://www.thebeatrice.com/), was named number nineteen, and [**The National Hotel**](https://www.blockislandhotels.com/?srsltid=AU7gw4XqZ4EhtnmNc4XbuKEcG3oGHGoopkBkzdRNZnPu7VDy0SctJTOu) on Block Island came in at number twenty.\n\nOcean House Collection’s three coastal Rhode Island properties have also earned top-five rankings in [**Condé Nast Traveler’s 2026 Readers’ Choice Awards**](https://www.cntraveler.com/inspiration/readers-choice-awards). [**Weekapaug Inn**](https://weekapauginn.com/) was named the number two resort in New England, followed by [**Ocean House**](https://www.oceanhouseri.com/) at number four, while [**Watch Hill Inn**](https://www.watchhillinn.com/) earned the number four hotel ranking in the Northeast.\n\n[**Castle Hill Inn**](https://www.castlehillinn.com/) will receive a Community Impact Award at Preserve Rhode Island’s 2026 Rhody Awards (October 25), recognizing the thoughtful restoration of its historic Agassiz Mansion following February’s fire.\n\n[**Nick-a-Nees**](https://www.instagram.com/_nickanees_/), a longtime neighborhood bar known for affordable drinks and live music without a cover charge, is struggling as rising property taxes, rent and operating costs threaten its future. With its liquor license renewal due Dec. 1, staff members have launched a **[GoFundMe fundraise](https://www.gofundme.com/f/help-keep-nickanees-alive-a-providence-institution)r** to cover critical expenses and keep the Providence gathering place open through the holidays.\n\nNewport Restaurant Group is marking thirty years of employee ownership this year, coinciding with Employee Ownership Month in October. Of NRG’s 1900 employees, approximately 1,100 are year-round with 850 added during peak season.\n\nThe 2027 volunteer schedule is now available for the [**Warm-A-Heart Gingerbread Program**](https://docs.google.com/forms/d/e/1FAIpQLSfsIyKVJw4UudUxNH3jlWuxPxmTlw8sU7k3aEy7xIDgLb1_fA/viewform). Join the team at Gracie’s and Ellie’s in helping Rhode Island families in need this holiday season, by assembling the Warm-A-Heart Gingerbread Kits. Tuesday, Thursday and Friday kit production will be located at  Refocus at 45 Greeley St. in Providence.\n\n**FARMERS MARKETS, GROCERIES AND DELIVERY**\n\n[**Garden City Center**](https://gardencitycenter.com/event/garden-city-center-farmers-market/2025-07-13/) is bringing back its weekly farmers market in partnership with Rhode Island Night Market. Visitors can enjoy the family- and dog-friendly market every Sunday while discovering just-picked produce, pantry staples, handmade goods and more for purchase from more than thirty vendors each week! The event series is free to attend and will be open rain or shine from 10 a.m. to 2 p.m. every Sunday through Oct. 18.\n\n[**The Farm Fresh RI Farmers Market**](https://farmfreshri.us11.list-manage.com/track/click?u=9e444aee4858503ab3d08b6d9&id=61bb2924cc&e=ae0ed5c1d1) is open Nov. 7, 2026-April 24, 2027 on Saturdays from 9 a.m.-1 p.m. at 10 Sims Avenue in Providence. Get to know various food and farm vendors (more than sixty-five of them, to be exact) at this farmers market. Don’t forget to visit the businesses that call Farm Fresh home, like New Harvest Coffee and Spirits, Anchor Toffee, Rhed’s Hot Sauce, Providence Brewing Co. and Wright’s Creamery.\n\nSupport agricultural life and local economy at the [**Aquidneck Growers Market**](https://guide.farmfreshri.org/food/farmersmarkets_details.php?market=1), every Saturday from 9 a.m.–noon. at Stoneacre Garden at 151 Swinburne Row in Newport, November 2 through April 26. Attendees can find an assortment of both organic and conventionally grown produce, fresh and ready for any recipe.\n\nThe [**Mount Hope Farm farmers market**](https://www.mounthopefarm.org/farmers-market) continues from 9 a.m. to 12:30 p.m. at Mount Hope Farm in Bristol each Saturday. Head over for your weekly groceries, courtesy of local farmers and artisans like Barden Family Orchard, Roots Farm, Brightside Seafood, Navad Bakers and Two Gals Cocktails. Need a dose of cuteness? Be sure to visit the farm animals during your visit. There are pre-ordering options as well as the ability to buy on site.\n\nThe [**Tiverton Farmers Market**](http://tivertonfarmersmarket.com) is moving inside for the season at Tiverton Middle School at 10 Quintal Dr. in Tiverton from Oct. 18, 2026 through May 16, 2027. Browse a selection of vendors offering local produce, meats, seafood, baked goods and more every Sunday from 10 a.m.–1:30 p.m. Support local musicians, artists, food entrepreneurs and nonprofit organizations as they gather to connect with community members. Vegan and vegetarian options are available.\n\n* [Facebook](http://www.facebook.com/sharer.php?u=https://www.rimonthly.com/diners-update-7/)\n*
